# Neptune stop notes

Lab notebook of the stop agent for `neptune`. Read `assets-src/shared/STOP-AGENT.md` first, then this.

## Log

- Start. Cloned, branch `agent/neptune`, merged `origin/main` at 9602aca. `npm ci` and `npm run build` pass. Rendered the stand-in `hold` and `terrain` comparisons at the draft tier and pushed them as the first looks.

## How the engine works (read once, kept here so it need not be read again)

- A stop = `src/stops/neptune/set.ts` (`createSet(ctx)` returns `StopSet { id, world, near?, sky?, update(state, shot, view), dispose }`) and `plan.ts` (`MoveParams`, `new StopPlan('neptune', fit, params)`). Mars is the pattern: `src/stops/mars/{set,plan,look,assets}.ts` with JSON beside them.
- Engine per sample: sky (with sky patches from `set.sky`, aim set by engine from `skyframe.json`), then `world` scene (set.world positioned at origin minus camera, rotated by `state.rotation`), then `near`, dust, cover. Post once on the average. Body pixels write alpha 1, sky 0 (G5 coverage). Diag views through `diagOut(...)` in `src/diagnostics/diag.ts`: texels (counted flag), layers, depth, flow.
- `state.view` is `orbit` or `terrain`. Terrain view: set.world origin = the spot on the globe, rotation = terrainFrame (x right, y normal, -z heading). Camera pose from `plan.terrain(t)`: position `(side*u, rise*u, -forward*u)` in camera heights around the fitted relief camera at origin, u from -0.5 to 0.5.
- Ending: `ending.retreat/lookback/fade` draw Neptune's set in the orbit view with `state.segment.stop !== plan.id`; Mars blends its light from `plan.lightDir` to `shot.sun.dir` (`lit`). LOOKBACK in `src/director/film.ts`: Neptune 31 deg off the Sun, distance 9 radii, vfov 34. So the crescent is seen at phase angle about 149 deg.
- Shared classes: `Planet` (`src/bodies/planet.ts`: wrap map, patch levels up to 6, storm turn about an axis by mask, light curve of 33 knots, ground haze, grazing haze), `HazeShell` (`src/bodies/haze.ts`), `MoonBody` (displaced, with baked lit map + mask), `Relief` (`src/terrain/relief.ts`: layers of color+alpha+dist with aprons, masks for mist/dust shift, valley fog march, air layer), `FarPoint`, `Cover` (engine-owned, set from plan colors).
- Approved pixels used as textures go through `inverseFilmic` and the lens vignette is undone on them (`uVignette`).
- Stand-in rings are a `RingGeometry` with 6 gaussian lines (shimmer risk). Neptune needs its own ring module.
- The loader counts texture bytes: w*h*4*(4/3 with mips), float data textures by byteLength. Limits: full 1400 MB, web 450 MB.
- Config: 30 fps. neptune.hold 151-156 s (frames 4530-4679, judged 4605), dive 156-159 (judged 4707), terrain 159-165 (judged 4860), rise 165-167.5 (judged 5006). Cover 0.5 s at 0.6 of dive, 0.4 of rise.

My own limb points: the fitted silhouette sits on the disc edge at the top; the hero limb is 6 to 10 px outside it at the right (0 deg) and upper left (110 to 140 deg). Accepted, known weakness.
- The hero has a thin bright rim line about 8 px outside the disc edge on the lit right limb, and a thin blue line on the night limb.
- Band axis from the streak orientation of the hero (structure tensor, score 0.97): `a = [0.29036982, 0.66913061, -0.68406842]` in fit coordinates (x right, y up, z toward camera). Sub-camera latitude about -36 deg. The -a pole is visible at lower left on the night side. Saved in `out/work/nep/bandaxis.npy` (untracked, value is here).
- Hints: sunDir `[0.71645, 0.697532, -0.012168]`; Sun in frame near hero px (2514, 227), dir `[0.346125, 0.175842, -0.921562]`; Triton hero circle (337, 407) r 95, position `[-5.671008, 2.437694, -12.56572]`, radius 0.475967, pale with pink upper part, lit from the right; ring normal `[0.086509, 0.99591, 0.026066]`, radius 1.4082 (low confidence; in the hero the ring is a faint wide dusty band with brighter thin arcs, lower left in front of the night side and at right); storm near hero (0.55, 0.50).
- Close image: the horizon runs from about (10, 300) at the left edge to about (2710, 670) at the right in the 2816x1536 close image (y from top), a tilted, gently curved limb with a wide blue haze band above the deck. The dark storm sits in the lower right foreground, the white band streams from the left edge round it.

## Design decisions

- Orbit: own `NeptunePlanet` shader in `src/stops/neptune/` (copy of `src/bodies/planet.ts`, changed). Body Y axis = band axis `a` (globe quaternion in `src/stops/neptune/fit.json`). Spin about it; with omega = +a the visible surface moves right and down along the streaks. `hold.axis` near `a`, orbit opposite to the spin. Ring keeps its fitted normal.
- One RGBA texture per level: RGB = cloud-free deck albedo (hero divided by the light curve, tone coded), A = cirrus opacity. Cirrus colour is a computed white. Alpha from luminance `(hero - deck)/(white - deck)`. WebP with alpha saved with `exact=True`. Loader does not premultiply.
- Memory plan (full): wrap 8192x4096 RGBA = 179 MB, five 4096 patch levels at 89.5 MB = 447 MB, total 626 MB, leaving about 770 MB for relief layers, Triton, sky patches, ring.
- Cirrus is sampled where the view ray crosses a shell at radius about 1.004 (parallax in the dive). It streams by an extra latitude-dependent rotation about body Y with bounded fine turbulence, all zero at the judged frame (`elapsed = time - holdMid`).
- Soft shadow computed in the shader: 4 to 6 taps of cirrus alpha offset toward the Sun (`L_tangent * h / max(ndl, 0.15)`), modest strength. Deck = hero pixels where there is no cloud, generated cloud-free deck (image tool: remove cirrus and shadows from the hero, colour matched) under and beside clouds.
- Storm turns by rotating the deck lookup about the storm centre with falloff, zero at the judged frame.
- Haze: ground haze in the shader, a thin shell with the bright rim line at about 1.008 R, a wide halo, one layer across the disc edge on lit and night side. Night side never black. Crescent checked from the lookback angle (phase about 149 deg).
- Triton: real sphere, generated map, moves about 2 px per frame. Ring: own true 3D module with a smooth analytic, filtered profile so it does not shimmer. Hero sky patch for the hold. Sun as a small star with spikes via the plan light settings.
- Terrain: close image projected from its own camera onto a fitted sphere deck plus a raised cloud layer (mesh displaced by a height map). Motion as rigid rotations on the sphere reprojected into image coordinates (storm about its centre with falloff, cloud band about the band pole). Cloud shadows as a soft shadow map taken from the close image (approved over clean deck) travelling with the clouds. Clean deck via image tool. Aprons outpainted left, right and bottom on one extended canvas. Haze/air layer at the horizon. Terrain sky patch. Fit the horizon to get camera height over radius and set `dive.altitude` to match. Fine detail added in the dark lower-left foreground keeping approved pixels as the low frequencies.
- Dive and rise: patch levels 2x apart designed with a predictor. Blue haze cover streaming on both sides, thinning over 10 frames or more. Descent grade toward the terrain light. Rise ends with the whole body in frame, calm.

## Notes from the Builder (03:15 UTC, day 1)

- `public/assets/neptune/web/` at or under 35 MB on disk (not 40). Full stays 150 MB. The film is rendered from full.
- Engine requests as early as possible (they move the engine hash for every stop). Prefer stop-local code.
- The set is also on screen in the travel before the hold (body grows from a point of light: `FarPoint` as in the Mars set) and after the rise. Keep both free of pops. Judged in Phase 5.
- Ending (`ending.retreat`, `lookback`, `fade`): camera pulls away past Triton at close range, turns back, Neptune a thin blue crescent, Sun one bright star. The crescent needs a glowing limb and a night side that is not pure black. Triton's map must hold up larger than in the hero. Judged in Phase 5.

## Geometry decided

- `src/stops/neptune/fit.json` globe quaternion = `[-0.3659887, 0.0456763, -0.1774456, 0.9124029]`: body Y = band axis, body Z = camera direction projected (lon 0 under the hold camera). Sub-camera lon 0, lat -36.3. Sub-solar lon 35.0, lat 43.1 (the visible south pole is in polar night). Storm at lon -0.25, lat -18.8.
- `assets-src/neptune/geo.py` holds the shared geometry and tone functions (decode = inverse filmic of sRGB to linear, encode = the reverse).
- Hero in scene-linear: night side about (0.0013, 0.007, 0.017); deck blue rises 0.09 at ndl 0.15 to 1.14 at ndl 0.75; deck R/B about 0.07, G/B 0.40 at the terminator to 0.24 at high sun; brightest cloud luminance about 1.2 for ndl > 0.4, 0.7 at 0.2 to 0.4, 0.25 at 0.15.

## Wrap pipeline (decided and measured, 03:15 UTC)

- Scripts: `assets-src/neptune/wrap/unproject.py` (2x hero to the band-aligned equirect window, `out/work/nep/wrap/hero-map.png` 4552x3641 with `hero-map-geo.npz`: mu, ndl, window), `curves.py` (deck and cloud light curves, `curves-measured.json`), `separate.py` (functions), `sepmap.py` (separation in map space, writes `hero-layers.npz`: deck albedo, alpha, weight).
- 2x hero: `tools/ml/upscale.py`, about 6 minutes, `checkmoved` PASS (PSNR 54.17 dB, SSIM 0.9989, flow p99 0.028 px).
- Deck albedo reference `A0 = (0.058, 0.215, 0.80)` scene-linear. scene = albedo * deckCurve(ndl), 33 knots over ndl -1..1. Night about (0.022, 0.034, 0.021), terminator 0.027/0.040/0.027, 0.27/0.33/0.25 at ndl 0.25, 0.71/0.81/0.79 at 0.5, 1.18/1.06/1.14 at 0.625, 1.58/1.14/1.32 at 1.
- Clouds are bluish in scene-linear: `K = (0.30, 0.39, 1.0)`. Cloud scene blue level by ndl knots `[-1,-0.2,0,0.06,0.125,0.19,0.25,0.31,0.375,0.44,0.5,0.56,0.69,1]` is `[0.02,0.02,0.05,0.15,0.35,0.77,1.26,1.7,2.0,2.3,2.5,2.7,3.0,3.2]`. The shader gets a second 33 knot curve for the cirrus.
- Separation: albedo = decode(map)/deckCurve; local floor (25th percentile) gives the deck hue; least squares unmix `C = p*hue + q*K`; zone m from `q/white`; deck = albedo outside the zone, anisotropic fill along longitude inside (normalized convolution at growing scales, clean pixels only, plus a grain tile); alpha from luminance. Recomposite error in the well lit part: mean 2.48 of 255, p99 37 (thin bright cloud cores). Telea inpainting was tried first and rejected (bright blobs, blur). First unmix with a white cloud colour left pale clouds in the deck, fixed with K.
- Hero-lit part of the sphere is only about lon -60..70, lat -47..35 (12 percent). Hero pixels fade out for mu < 0.42 and ndl < 0.2. Everything else is generated.
- Far side plan: separate layers. Deck tiles (cloud free, flat lit, band aligned) and cirrus tiles (white on black, alpha directly). Mid band: 3 tiles of 160 x 90 deg at lon 60, 180, 300. Poles: polar projection caps of radius 55 deg, north and south. All upscaled 2x. Detail of each tile kept as ratio to its low pass, colour set from a zonal target profile so tiles cannot differ in colour. Cross-fade overlaps (cirrus by max).
- Limb in the hero (sRGB): thin bright line at the disc edge, strongest at theta 45 to 90 deg (R to 240), into the twilight (theta 120 B 169, 135 B 55, 150 B 33), gone at 165 to 180. Outside glow at theta 45: B 235 at +3 px, 209 at +11, 166 at +25, 123 at +40, 85 at +70. Inside the sun side limb brightens over the last 20 to 30 px. Night side inside about (2, 6, 17). Plan: ground haze in the shader, thin bright core shell plus wide halo, forward scatter term (zero at the hold, strong in the lookback crescent).

## First real render (03:35 UTC)

- Wrap assembled by `assets-src/neptune/wrap/assemble.py`: `public/assets/neptune/full/wrap.webp` 8192x4096 RGBA (RGB = sRGB of albedo x 0.5, A = cirrus opacity, WebP exact, quality 92, 9.7 MB), web 4096x2048 (2.4 MB). Far side from generated tiles (deck: `deck-m0`, `deck-m1b`, `deck-m2` at lon 60, 180, 300, caps `deck-n`, `deck-s`; cirrus `cir-m0..m5`, `cir-n`, `cir-s`), all take 1 except `deck-m1` (bowed bands, replaced by `deck-m1b`). No ML upscale of the tiles. Tile detail = log ratio to an anisotropic low pass, colour from the hero zonal median profile so tiles cannot differ in colour.
- Stop code now: `planet.ts` (NeptunePlanet: deck + cirrus on a raised shell with parallax, two shadow taps toward the Sun, storm turn, stream shear, ground haze, rim and forward terms), `triton.ts` (sphere with map), `ring.ts` (true 3D annulus, analytic ringlets widened by `fwidth`, planet shadow, additive, alpha 0), `plate.ts` (temporary terrain plate), `look.ts`, `haze.json`, `assets.ts`, `set.ts`.
- First hold comparison at the draft tier: framing, storm, cirrus and terminator are in place. Wrong so far: no hero sky patch yet, the limb shell is far too bright and wide, the ring is too bright, too sharp and not on the hero's ellipse, the Sun is too small, clouds read slightly lavender.
- Known open problem: the hero inset carries the hero's limb lighting, so in a flat lit preview it shows as a lighter lens. To check from the rise and lookback angles.
- Triton map: `assets-src/neptune/triton/make.py` from `out/work/nep/gen/triton-map-t1.png` (take 1), 4096x2048, seam mirror-blended, poles smoothed to the zonal mean.

## Orbit view work, 03:45 to 04:20 UTC

- Own atmosphere module `src/stops/neptune/air.ts`: three exponential layers plus one thin line shell, each with its own aimed light direction (`aim`, scaled by `home`) and `away` values `[from, to, gain]` that replace `dayFrom`, `dayTo` and the day gain when `home` is 0 (ending, far side). `home` is 1 near the hold view and 0 in the ending. Values in `src/stops/neptune/haze.json` (`air`, `limb`).
- `check/fit_air.py` (least squares on the outside limb profiles) was unstable. Values were set by hand from it. The dim inside limb was extinction by optically thick layers: depths bounded, `extinction` 0.5.
- Veil: the hero deck brightens toward the sunward limb (1.3 to 2 times at mu 0.45 to 0.25, about 3 times near the subsolar limb). The planet shader mixes toward `limb.veil` by `veilAmount * (1 - smoothstep(veilMu, mu)) * pow(smoothstep(veilSun.xy, dot(n, veilDir)), veilSun.z)`; `veilDir` is the Sun aimed by `veilAim` (toward theta 25 deg, because the hero's brightening is stronger at the right limb than at the top). `wrap/sepmap.py` divides the same veil out of the hero inset (reads `haze.json`), so after any veil change run `sepmap.py` then `assemble.py` (about 5 min).
- Limb terms added 04:10: `limb.shade` (air on the disc is switched off where the ground is unlit, except at mu < 0.1, times `home`; it removed a blue wash in the upper left twilight zone), `limb.twilight` (limb darkening for ndl 0 to 0.4, the hero is 0.5 times the curve there), `limb.dark` now applied after the air, direction theta -30 deg, wider with strength (the hero has a wide dark blue band inside a thin cyan line at the lower right limb, hero B 0.06 to 0.09 from rt 0.945 at theta -15 to -20), times `home`. `rim` raised to `[2.2, 4.4, 6]`, width 0.055 (hero rim line peaks 3 to 5 at rt 0.993 to 0.995). Hot spot layer `dayFrom` 0.9. `look.nightGain` 1.2 on the night knots of the deck curve (hero night 0.014 to 0.019 B, film was 0.012 to 0.016).
- The hero limb is not a circle: disc edge at rt 0.996 at theta 45 to 90 (inside the fitted sphere by 4 to 5 hero px), at rt 1.004 at theta 0, cyan line at rt 1.013 to 1.017 at theta -15 to -20, line at 1.005 to 1.007 at theta 135. Probably an oblate body (minor axis near theta 65 deg). The sphere is kept. Known weakness: limb offset up to 4 px at 1920x1080.
- Ring: fitted to hand-picked points of the hero's main strand (`out/work/nep/ringfit3.py`): `normal [0.1681, 0.9779, 0.1244]`, `centre [0.0533, 0.0532, -0.4902]` (rms 12.7 px; a centred ring gives 73 px). `centre` is scaled by `home`. Main strand radius 1.52. Hero strand points: far branch (95,1010) to (470,795), near branch (100,1050), (250,1195), (460,1260), (700,1365), (760,1385), right (2440,1160) to (2800,1395). Still open: the right branch is too faint and too regular in the film.
- Sky patch for the hold: `assets-src/neptune/sky/patch.py hold HERO FILL GLOW OUT`. Sky = hero minus a film frame rendered with the sky off (`sky.json` gain 0, review tier, copied to `out/work/nep/sky/glow-only.png`), hero's own sky from 1.07 radii out except where the film glow covers it, Triton's disc and the projected ring annulus; never excluded within 330 px of the Sun. Elsewhere a generated fill (`out/work/nep/sky/fill-t1.png`, take 1 of 6; recipe to be written in `sky/RECIPE.md`), low frequencies matched to the hero; outside the disc the fill's stars are replaced by the hero's own stars (top hat by line openings, so ring strands and the limb line are not copied), inside the disc the fill's stars are scaled to the hero's star energy. Margin 0.125: mirrored sky with the Sun removed (radial glow profile continued), blended over 110 px into a plain mirror at the border. Lossless WebP 3520x1980 (6.3 MB), web 1760x990 (1.9 MB). `sky.json` gain 1.538 = 1 / skyExposure. The engine draws the Sun core as a black dot in the patch.
- Triton: light aimed by `look.triton.aim` (times `home`). Film Triton measured 89 px radius against 95 in the hero, 8 px up and left (to check at the final tier).
- Check scripts: `check/profiles.py` (fine limb profiles), `check/outside.py` (compact table by theta), `check/ratio.py STILL OUT` (film over hero map in blue, red = film brighter, and a table of medians by ndl for mu > 0.45). At 04:05 the disc medians matched within 3 percent for ndl 0.1 to 0.8.
- Engine batch 1 merged (b4ae65c): sky patch `weight(time, view)` optional, faster sky pass. Software rasterizer computes both sides of an `if`; put costly conditional work in `for (int k = 0; k < (cond ? 1 : 0); k++)` with `textureGrad`.

## Orbit view work, 04:20 to 05:10 UTC

- Line shell (`air.ts`, `haze.json` `air.line`): `bulge [0.866,-0.496,0.0645,0.011]` (shell radius raised toward theta -30, times `home`), `tail [0.5,2.8,5.0]` with `tailEdge [0.3,0.75]` (extra cyan light on the line by `dot(tp, sun)`, times `home`), `inner 1.0005` (closed a dark gap at the night limb), `lift 0.0025`. Broad layer night colour `[0.0005,0.0016,0.0036]`.
- Twilight cirrus: `sepmap.py` builds the 33 knot cloud curve itself (`CLOUD_KNOTS/CLOUD_VALUES`, tint by sun angle `TINT_KNOTS/TINT`: neutral grey at the terminator to `(0.30,0.39,1)` above ndl 0.3) and writes `src/stops/neptune/curve.json`. Alpha is extracted down to ndl -0.06. `gweight` (mu 0.08..0.26, ndl -0.1..-0.04) removes generated cirrus inside the hero's disc. `planet.ts` cloud fade `smoothstep(-0.09, -0.03, ndlc)`. Result: grey-white twilight cirrus like the hero.
- Limb field: `field.json` 40x40 x 2 sides (side 1 = clockwise from the Sun direction), mu 0..1 by ndl -0.3..1, a log2 gain per cell, applied after the air, weighted by `home * look.field.amount`. One iteration: `npm run still -- --frame 4605 --tier review && python3 assets-src/neptune/check/field.py out/work/stills/f04605-16x9-review-full.png`. Rules in `field.py`: `EDGE 3`, `RIM 8, CAP 1.2`, `hot` (ndl above 0.84..0.93: columns below 8 clipped to +-0.4). Residual now rms log2 0.256, p95 0.568.
- Limb terms in `planet.ts`: rim centred at `rimMu` (times `home`) and a cut at the very edge (`cut`, `cutMu`, `cutFrom`, `cutTo`, `cutAim`). `cut` 0.75 drew a dark outline at the lit limb in crops; it is 0.3 now and the outline is gone. The right limb still shows two or three thin stripes where the hero has one soft band and one thin cyan line.
- Ring: `ring/map.py` measures the ring map from the hero (hero minus a still with the ring off, unwrapped into the fitted ring plane); a second pass adds the broad residual from a still with the first map. `STRAND 2.2`, `FLOOR 0.0008`. Annulus medians within about 5 percent of the hero by azimuth. The left ansa in front of the night side is fainter than the hero's (crop D).
- Triton: `make.py` `SATURATION 0.3, CONTRAST 1.25, WASH (0.93,0.84,0.845), PLAIN (0.97,0.975,1.0)`; `triton.ts` relief step `eps = max(9/mapWidth, 2.5*length(fwidth(d)))`; `look.triton.gain 1.75`. In the final compare Triton is a little smaller (89 against 95 px radius in the hero frame), darker and more speckled pink than the hero's.
- Sun: radial blue medians round the Sun equal the hero's at the review tier, rays the same.
- Engine batches 1 to 3 merged (main 3523b45, engine hash e44fa21a5a599a98). `skyAim.orbit [25.78793, 56.89437, -50.63077]` is in `plan.ts` (printed by `npm run -s aim -- --stop neptune`; the hold is the same picture). Do not tune it for the ending.
- `npm run budget -- --stop neptune` throws in `tools/budget.ts:18` (`travel.firstFrame` of undefined: Neptune has no travel segment). Reported to the Builder. Texture memory by hand: wrap 179 MB, Triton 44.7, sky patch 37.2, ring under 3, stand-in plate about 23: about 290 MB of 1400.
- Builder at 05:03: motion first. Orbit milestone sent as it is; limb, ring and Triton refinements come back through the rounds. Move to the terrain view, then dive and rise.

## Terrain view (05:20 to 06:00 UTC)

Technique: the approved close image projected from its fitted camera onto a fitted sphere, split into deck, raised cloud layer and shadow factor. Class `Closeup` in `src/stops/neptune/closeup.ts`, numbers in `closeup.json`, fit in `close.json` (written by `terrain/horizon.py 30`).

- Fit: vfov 30 (the horizon does not constrain it; 30 keeps the hold's lens), pitch 20.627 down, roll -9.539, dip 12.409, radius 41.806 camera heights, nadir `[-0.155098, -0.922955, -0.352277]`, rms 0.029 degrees. Plan values: `horizonDeg 8.22, dipDeg 12.41, rollDeg 9.54`.
- Canvas (`terrain/cgeo.py`): the same camera with 1.75 times the tangent range, top edge shared with the approved frame. Approved box on a 4096x2304 canvas: x 878 to 3218, y 0 to 1317.
- Shader: one back side sphere of radius R(1 + glowTop) at the centre `nadir * (R + 1)`. Per pixel: ray against the deck sphere and the cloud slab (`cloudBase 0.07`, `cloudThickness 0.06` camera heights). Deck = core map inside the approved box, wide map outside, times the shadow factor read at the cloud advected point. Cloud found by a 10 step march through the slab against the height map with a secant step. Flow: clouds turn about the band axis (`streamPerSecond 0.035` heights per second, `streamShear 3`), the deck turns about the storm centre (`stormDegPerSecond 2`, falloff 1/(1+x^4), `stormRadius 1.1`). All zero at the judged time so frame 4860 is the approved image. Rays that miss the deck read the glow map, alpha 0. Expensive parts sit in `for` loops per the profiler finding, textures read with `textureGrad`.
- Maps: `terrain/layers.py` (see RECIPE). Canvas take 1 `gpt_image_2_6_flare` kept (centre aligned to 0.7 px), take 2 `seedream_5` rejected. Clean plate `clean-canvas-t1` kept, `clean-centre-t1` rejected. 2 of 6 takes used for each.
- Tone match of the apron: histogram match per channel inside the box, then log gain at the border carried outward at three blur sizes. Before that a plain gain clipped at 0.5 left a visible box seam (the generated canvas is far lighter than the approved image in the dark lower left).
- Sky: `sky/terrain.py` writes `close-glow.png` and `sky-terrain.webp`. The terrain set turns with the planet (`spinDegPerSecond 1.3`), so stars move about 4 degrees against the horizon each way during the view: the patch must be clean below the horizon. Mirrored about the horizon, no line left at the horizon.
- Plan: `terrain { vfov 30, forward 0.84, rise 0.31, side 0.05 }` is level flight of 0.9 camera heights along the view heading (level direction in camera axes `(0.0584, 0.3474, -0.9359)`). `dive.altitude 0.048` = 2 camera heights at the cover centre with `entryHeight 1.5` (g at the cover centre is 0.66).
- First results: judged frame equals the approved image in the compare at draft tier; sheet shows steady forward movement, the band comes toward the camera, the storm grows.
- Open: faint tone step where the approved band meets the generated band at the left box edge (visible in the first frames); below the mirrored strip the sky patch is flat without stars (only seen if the horizon drops more than about 7 degrees); the flow of the clouds has not been checked on its own yet; the dark lower left foreground has little detail.
- Sharpness (05:50): G17 at the judged terrain frame was 0.677 with the plain maps, lowest toward the bottom of the frame (0.5 to 0.6), 0.9 to 1.1 near the horizon: the loss is the motion blur of the forward flight. `layers.py` now adds the band the gate measures (blur 0.6 minus blur 2.0 film pixels) to the saved maps, gain `CRISP 1.0 / distance in camera heights`, at most 0.6. Result: G17 0.930, p25 0.889. Crops beside the approved image show no halo.
- G13 every 30th frame: all hold and terrain frames pass; dive frame 4710 fails (24 percent soft) until the patch levels exist.
- Dive plan (decided 05:55): `swoop: true` as on Mars, so the orbit camera reaches 2 camera heights over the deck at the cover centre with the terrain view's horizon and heading. Altitude at the last frame before the cover (4726) is 0.069 radii, looking 31 degrees down. Gnomonic patch levels 96, 48, 24, 12 degrees at 4096 from the wrap for G13 (the gate takes the larger of the two screen gradients, so an oblique view needs less than a nadir view). On top of that the terrain layers are drawn on the globe near the cover (the closeup sphere is the globe at 1 camera height = 1/41.8 radii), fading in below altitude 0.8, so the storm and the band the terrain view shows are on screen before the cover.

## Dive and rise (06:00 UTC on)

- Patch levels: six gnomonic levels round the dive spot (hero pixel 0.55, 0.52 = lon 0.23, lat -20.17), widths 96, 64, 40, 26, 16, 10 degrees at 4096 (web 2048), RGBA like the wrap (deck colour + cirrus opacity). `assets-src/neptune/patches/bake.py` writes them and `src/stops/neptune/patches.json`. Why six: with 96, 48, 24, 12 the dive passed G13 but rise frames 4988 to 4994 failed (3 to 9 percent soft), because the frame at altitude 0.4 reaches past the 24 degree level and the 48 degree level gives 0.9 texels per pixel there. Fade thresholds 1.1 to 1.6 texels per pixel of the parent.
- Detail: the wrap is 4 to 15 times too coarse for the last second before the cover. A 24 degree picture round the storm was generated from the wrap resample (prompt `prompts/patch-24.js`, take 1 `seedream_5` kept: same layout to 0.1 px median at 512 px, real cirrus filaments and spiral striations in the storm; take 2 `nano_banana_pro` returned the input unchanged). A second picture for the inner 12 degrees from the result (`prompts/patch-12.js`). `bake.py` matches each take to the wrap at low frequency (gain in linear light, blur 1/48 of the width), takes cloud opacity from the red channel against the wrap's deck, corrects its low frequencies to the wrap's opacity, divides the cloud out for the deck and uses the wrap's deck under thick cloud. Below the takes' own resolution it adds streak noise octaves (stretched 6 to 1 east to west) so that no level is a plain blow up.
- The level fetch in `planet.ts` is a zero or one pass `for` loop (profiler finding), six `if` blocks would all be computed.
- Cloud stream in the orbit view: the stream angle now follows time only within 2.6 s of the hold middle and eases to a stop 1.6 s later (`look.stream`). Reason: the cloud layer is sampled at the streamed direction, so with an unbounded stream the fine levels of the cloud layer sat 8 degrees (dive) to 20 degrees (rise) away from the camera at the cover.
- `rise.ahead 0.05` (was the dive's 0.14): the rise starts 2.9 degrees past the spot, so the frame after the cover stays inside the fine levels.
- Render time (06:25): with six levels a dive still near the cover took 120 to 200 s at the review tier (about 50 s before the patches). `layerAt` in `planet.ts` now computes every level weight first and fetches only the levels (and the wrap) whose final contribution is above zero, each inside a zero or one pass loop with `textureGrad`. Frame 4722: 65.8 s, pixel difference against the old shader mean 0.000, max 1 of 255.
- Cover (06:30): first values in `plan.ts`: `cover [0.3,0.5,0.98]`, `coverLow [0.05,0.13,0.42]`, `coverTerrain [0.42,0.62,1]`, `coverLowTerrain [0.035,0.08,0.26]`, `coverTop [0.3,0.06]`, `coverEdge [0.2,0.4]`, `coverEdgeRise [0.4,0.45]`, `coverShell [1.03,1.1]`, `coverRush` like Mars with lower contrast. To be judged on the cover sheets of the dive and the rise.

## Dive and rise, second pass (06:35 to 07:00 UTC)

- Draft tier sheets take 3 s per frame (review 60 to 80 s near the deck). Use `--tier draft` for motion and cover sheets, review only for judged stills.
- G13, every dive frame outside the cover (4680 to 4725 and the terrain frames of the dive): 0 soft pixels on all of them (`out/work/nep/g13-dive.log`). The cover window is 4726 to 4741 (centre 4734) for the dive and 4972 to 4987 (centre 4980) for the rise.

The orbit side starts level along the heading with the terrain view's horizon and roll, so the cover's pale top band and the horizon carry across (without `level` the first orbit frame looked straight down and the cover jumped from pale top to plain blue at 4979 to 4980). Altitude 0.06 at 4980, 0.29 at 4984, 0.85 at 4988, 3.4 at 5000, 4.03 at 5006, 4.2 at 5024: the whole body is in frame from about 4997 and nearly still for the last 20 frames.
- Cover edges: `coverEdge [0.2, 0.5]`, `coverEdgeRise [0.5, 0.45]` (the cover must be opaque at the centre frame, so 0.5 of the 16 frame window is the most one side can thin over).
- Tone bridge (`look.near`, `uNear` in `planet.ts`): the hero globe is electric blue, the approved close image is steel navy with neutral white cloud. Below altitude 0.45 the globe's surface colour blends toward a desaturated, darker, higher contrast version (saturation 0.3 toward a 0.3/0.5/0.2 weighted grey, power 1.4 about pivot 0.3, tint 0.75/0.95/1, gain 0.55 in the dive and 0.8 in the rise), full at altitude 0.05. Medians of the lower half, dive 4722 against terrain 4744 (sRGB): before (5, 34, 92) with the first try, now (16, 39, 74) against (29, 60, 94). At the judged dive frame 4707 (altitude 0.37) the blend is 12 percent.
- Ring away from the hold view: the measured ring map carries the hero's noise and a broad glow, which from the rise angle read as smoky blotches. `ring.ts` now blends by `home` between the measured map and three analytic strands (`look.ring.strands`: radius, width, level; widened to 0.8 pixel with the energy kept, so no shimmer) plus the faint sheet.
- Terrain seam at the left box edge: the step is in the generated canvas itself (the model painted the outside lighter and its clouds greyer, starting exactly at the box border), so matching inside the box could not remove it. `terrain/layers.py` `seam()` now measures the step in log tone and the ratio of local contrast between a 28 px strip inside and outside the border, and applies both to the outside with an exponential reach of 900 px.
- Cover colours (07:00): `cover [0.27,0.43,0.8]`, `coverLow [0.03,0.075,0.22]`, `coverTerrain [0.4,0.56,0.85]`, `coverLowTerrain [0.03,0.065,0.19]`. Frame 4729 at draft is navy with a pale top band, close to the bridged pre-cover frame and to the terrain view. The first values were too bright and saturated.
- `look.near.altitudeRise [0.05, 1]`: the rise climbs fast, so the bridge is spread over a taller range than in the dive. Frames 4984 (altitude 0.29) and 4986 (0.52) are a lighter blue than the terrain view; the change happens under the cover.
- G13, every rise frame 4950 to 5024 (`out/work/nep/g13-rise.log`): 0.00 percent soft on all, failed frames 0. Every dive frame outside the cover: 0.00 percent soft (`g13-dive.log`).
- Ending at draft (5080, 5140, 5235, 5320): glowing blue crescent, night side dark navy with faint structure, thin ring strands, Triton a pale crescent at close range, Sun a star with the Milky Way. No pop 5024 to 5030. Faint concentric banding on the night side at draft tier.
- Judged frames at review: 4707 (dive) sharp deck with the storm and the band under the motion blur; 5006 (rise) whole body in frame, storm and cirrus readable, three thin ring strands.
- Terrain sheet at draft (4770 to 4949): steady forward movement, the storm grows about a fifth and moves to the centre, the band comes toward the camera and streams.
- Still open at the box border (found 07:02 in frame 4744): where the camera is higher than the fitted camera it sees past the fitted horizon; those points read the map mirrored about the horizon, and there the core map (blue limb strip) and the wide map (paler) differ, so a pale wedge about 100 by 80 px sits at the right frame edge under the limb in the last second of the dive (4742 to 4769). Gone by 4770. Fix idea: match the wide map to the box as a profile of distance below the horizon, near both box borders.

## After milestone 3 (07:07 UTC on)

Milestone 3 sent at 07:06 on 956d8b4. Builder's order for what is left, worst first: (2) the pale wedge at the right edge 4742 to 4769; (3) limb colour step before the cover and the lighter blue 4984 to 4988 (colour and exposure must carry across both covers); (5) the fast first 10 frames of the rise; (6) ring strands a fifth down; (4) cover full within the first fifth of its window and thinning over the rest, if my controls allow it, no config change; (1) leave the heading: direction of travel, horizon line, colour and exposure must match across the cover, the layout of storm and band need not.

- (2) Cause found with `--diag layers` and crops of the maps: not the mirrored strip (that is only about 4 px tall on screen) but the wide deck map itself. Right of the box border the generated limb is a thin bright cyan line with the white haze starting sooner, the approved image has a soft blue band. A camera above the fitted one sees that strip stretched, so the difference reads as a wedge. `seam()` could not fix it: it carries the border step outward along rows, and the horizon slopes 9.5 degrees across the rows. Fix: `limb()` in `terrain/layers.py` (see RECIPE): profile below the horizon, read along the horizon, taken from the box and applied outside with a reach of 700 px. Log line: `limb correction p5 p50 p95 -0.472 -0.004 0.364`. Crops `out/work/nep/limb-after.jpg`.
- (3) Dive side: `uAirNear` in `air.ts` (shared with the planet shader): the air's light is multiplied toward `look.air.near [0.22, 0.45, 0.9]` by the same `near` amount as the tone bridge, so the broad teal halo of the hero thins to a dim blue line near the deck like the terrain limb. Rise side: `look.near.altitudeRise [0.4, 2.6]`, `gainRise 0.6` (were [0.05, 1] and 0.8).
- (5) `rise.settle 7` (was 5): altitude 0.48 at 4984, 0.91 at 4986, 1.45 at 4988, 2.04 at 4990, 3.95 at 5000, 4.15 at 5006, 4.20 at 5024.
- (6) `look.ring.strands` levels times 0.8.
- (4) `coverEdgeRise [0.4, 0.5]` (was [0.5, 0.45]): the reveal side of the rise now thins over 8 frames, the most the 16 frame window allows with an opaque centre frame. The dive already has `coverEdge [0.2, 0.5]`. Ten frames would need a haze veil of my own in the first frames after the cover; not built.
- After `limb()` the right wedge is gone in 4742 and 4746 (`out/work/nep/wedge-after.jpg`). Left border: a pale vertical stripe about 25 px wide below the limb, stronger toward the cloud band. Cause: the shadow factor (green of `close-height.png`) had a hard step at the left box border (about 0.61 outside against 1 inside, because the generated canvas is darker than the clean plate there and the split read that as shadow). The shader reads the shadow factor at the cloud's streamed position, so away from the judged time the step slides off the border and leaves a stripe of deck that was divided by 0.61 but is no longer shaded. Fix: `level()` in `layers.py` lifts the outside shadow factor by the border step in log, reach 900 px, so the factor is continuous across the border (the darkness stays in the deck map; the recomposite is unchanged).
- Draft stills after the changes (`out/work/nep/q3-a.jpg`): 4722 limb is a thin dim blue line (was a broad teal halo); 4984 and 4986 are a medium blue, 4988 lighter, 4990 and 4994 the hero blue; whole body in frame at 4994.
- 07:24: `level()` moved to the raw ratio (before the blurs); applied to the final factor it left a notch inside the box. After the rerun the green of `close-height.png` across the border (x 439, rows 150 to 350, every 3 px) reads `216 215 215 214 213 212 209 | 209 215 224 230 232 233 233`: no step. Log: `shade step p5 p50 p95 -0.092 0.355 0.938`, `wide recomposite error mean 0.20 p99 6`, `core recomposite error mean 0.25 p99 6`. Maps copied into both sets.
- Draft lower half medians (R G B): 4970 `84 132 175`, 4984 `74 112 178`, 4986 `86 127 193`, 4988 `57 110 188`, 4990 `32 95 172`. Exposure carries across the rise cover; the hue moves from the grey blue of the terrain plate to the hero blue over six frames.
- Cover sheets at draft (`out/work/sheets/neptune.rise-cover-16x9-draft.png`, `neptune.dive-cover-16x9-draft.png`): rise opaque 4976 to 4981, reveal 4982 to 4986 with the haze left behind as an arc over the limb; dive opaque to 4734, reveal 4735 to 4740.
- 07:26: at 4746 a pale stripe about 35 px wide still stood at the left border under the cloud band (`out/work/nep/left-after.jpg`). Cause: the clean plate (the deck under the clouds) stepped at the border (mean of the deck map rows 200 to 300: 47 outside, 32 to 36 inside), hidden by the cloud at the judged time and uncovered when the cloud streams on. Fix: `level()` now takes strip, blur along the border, reach and clip range; the plate is knitted per channel before alpha and the ratio (strip 3, reach 160), the finished shadow factor is knitted too (strip 6, reach 90). Border steps of the shadow factor above 12 of 255: 53 rows before, 3 after.

## Right limb, read from the final hold compare (07:45, for the first round)

Crops `out/work/nep/limbH.jpg` (lower right, 3x) and `limbF.jpg` (upper right, 2.4x), approved left, film right.
- Lower right (position angle 0 to -25 from the disc centre, x to the right, y up): the hero's disc darkens softly toward the limb, then one thin cyan line, then a soft glow. The film shows three stripes: a light blue stripe (the sphere's own rim), a dark blue gap, and a teal band with a hard outer edge. Cause: `air.line.bulge` lifts the line shell 0.011 radii (7.4 px) off the sphere toward theta -30 to follow the hero's outline, which is not a circle. The sphere's limb stays where it is, so both are seen. Plan: bulge 0 (the Builder dropped outline fits of a few pixels), then refit `field.json` once.
- Upper right (angle 15 to 55): blue equals the hero within 3 levels from 20 px inside to 34 px outside the limb, but red and green are 20 to 35 levels higher in the first 12 px outside the white rim (at angle 35, 4 to 14 px outside: R 169 148 125 118 100 against 145 113 96 89 79). That is the line shell (inner 1.0005, outer 1.0065, lift 0.0025, with `tail [0.5, 2.8, 5.0]` at full strength where `dot(tp, sun)` is above 0.75). The hero has its crisp cyan line only where `dot(tp, sun)` is below about 0.65. Plan: fade the tail out again above about 0.8, check the profile, keep blue.

- 08:34: 38dd59d committed, main 234a4c2 merged as 212fd01, pushed, looks pushed 08:33, note to the Builder. Round 9 was mirrored from cf266da (`cb8a9c2 Round 9: neptune from agent/neptune at cf266da`), so everything after it is judged in my second round. Quick measure on 212fd01 (every 10th frame, 413 s): `PASS G13 neptune: 0 of 47 frames over 0.02 soft`, `PASS G17 neptune: 4605 median 1.188 p25 1.045 over 65 tiles; 4860 median 0.929 p25 0.887 over 75 tiles`.
- Read `scorecards/round-005/SUMMARY.md` (Mars, last round) to see what the critics name. R1 majors there: the sunlit limb "terminates in a hard, stair-stepped aliased circular cutout against the outer glow", the night limb "a razor-sharp dark geometric cutout slicing abruptly into the starfield". R4 majors: both covers read as a concealed cut or a blur dissolve. R3: the far side "melted and smeared", no soft terminator. So the limb profile is not a small matter for the judged hold.
- 08:45: the striped right limb, real cause. `field.json` oscillated in the rim bins: neighbours at -2.2 and +1.2 log2 (side 0, ndl 0.69: -1.73 -1.37 -0.36 -2.2 +1.2 -0.65), a factor 10 between stripes 1.7 px wide. The hero's outline and the film's sphere differ by 3 to 5 px along the right and left limb (the film's silhouette against the fitted unit sphere, from a `--diag layers` still: tangent radius 1.0045 at angle 15, 1.0025 at 90, 0.9965 at 190; the hero's cyan line sits at 1.012 at angle -15), so a pixel by pixel ratio in bins that fine cannot converge, each refit pushed the stripes further to the caps.
- Fix 1, `check/field.py`: the first 8 mu bins are the field smoothed along mu (sigma `SOFT = 2` bins) and held within `-DEEP..CAP` (`-1.4..0.6` log2) of the interior value (mean of bins 8 to 10), blended back over bins 7 to 10. The `hot` clamp is gone. The stripes were first removed by setting the rim bins to the interior value, then two refits.
- Fix 2, `planet.ts`: `uMelt` (`limb.melt [0.12, 0.04, 1]` in `haze.json`: width in mu in the hold view, width away from it, amount). Below that mu the disc is mixed toward the air of the ray that just misses the globe at the same position angle (`air(tangent, false)` times the same gain), inside a `for` loop so pixels away from the limb skip it. - 09:05: cause of the hard cirrus found. `wrap/sepmap.py` took the cirrus opacity from a luminance with 0.3 blue. In the hero the blue channel is at 255 wherever the cirrus is bright, the inverse tone curve turns that into 4.0, so the opacity jumped to 1 in every bright core (19 percent of cloud pixels above 0.95) and the soft body of a streak broke into hard flecks. Recomposite error against the hero inside clouds: 12.8 of 255. Fix: least squares over red and green only (`FIT = [0.5, 0.5, 0]`): error inside clouds 5.5, share above 0.95 4.6 percent, the streak is one soft body again (`out/work/nep/wrap-crop4.jpg`: hero, recomposite, opacity; top row before, bottom row after). Neither the shadow (set to 0 as a test) nor a wider footprint for the opacity (2 and 3.5, new `clouds.soft`, left at 1) changed the look, so the map was the cause.

- 19:48 to 19:55, item 2, final design ("D3", in `plan.ts`): `NeptunePlan.dive()` overrides with the public API only (`super.dive`, `coverCenter`, `coverWindow`). `export const descent = { onset: 3, pin: 4707.25, settleDeg: 2, settlePower: 2.2 }`. Before the cover's first frame (join 4726) the engine's own path is flown at a warped time (bisection, 36 steps, cached per time) so that `ln(h0 / h) = drift * t + accel * ramped(t).s + late term`: the acceleration ramps in over 3 frames from the hold's own drift, is constant to 4707.25 where the fall speed equals the engine's own at that frame (`accel = (pace - drift) / ramped(knee).v`), then a power term reaches the engine's height and speed at the join. From the join the engine as it is. After the cover centre an extra pitch of 2 degrees times x^2.2 carries the last dive frames' pitch rate into the terrain. Solved: h0 2.07236, drift 0.000352, accel 5.368e-4, late 0.08523, power 1.765; warp monotone, largest shift -3.9 frames at 4703.25.

(1) long thin isolated strokes, half width 2.7 px median, hundreds to thousands of texels long; (2) feathered tufts that hang together in chains 1500 to 2700 texels long. At 5006 one wrap texel is 0.4 px, so both read as straight strokes 200 to 1000 px long. Trial 1 (softer read, thinned by the flow map) left their length alone, which is why they stayed strokes.

Red = what stays of the wrap's alpha: 1 inside the hero's own cloud zone (1 - `gweight`, soft 24 px), elsewhere `near * clump`; `near` = 1 within 60 px of a thick body (alpha opened with a 4 px disc, over 0.25) falling to 0 at 160 px, so thin strokes with no tuft beside them go; `clump` = smooth step of a noise drawn out along the flow (208 by 72 px), which cuts the chains into pieces with soft ends. Green (coded times 4) = the removed thin strokes blurred 16 by 6 px, times a patchy factor: soft wisps where the strokes were.

- 02:31 to 02:38, far side cirrus, take 5 of 6 designed offline during chain A (`assets-src/neptune/wrap/far.py`, written to `out/work/nep/cirrus/t5/`, not in `public/` yet).
  - The wrap's alpha at native size (`out/work/nep/cirrus/t5/iso-0.jpg`, `iso-1.jpg`): the feathers are bundles of thin fibres, so a test by stroke width throws them out with the scratches (that was take 4's fault). What tells a scratch from a feather is that it stands alone: alpha blurred 3 texels over alpha blurred 12 texels is about 4 on a lone line and about 1.3 inside a bundle. `lone = step(ratio, 1.9, 2.8)`, widened 9 texels and blurred 3: takes out 6 percent of the alpha (lone lines and dashes), the feathers stay.
  - The map now holds three fields and NO blurred wisps: red = 1 - far * lone; green = a slow latitude offset (noise 72 by 28 texels sigma, stored as 127 + 127 * n / 3, neutral on the hero's side) to bend the chains; blue = far * 0.9 * U + (1 - far), U = a noise 208 by 72 texels sigma taken to a uniform spread, so the shader can cut the chains at any share with soft ends and give the pieces different opacity. On the hero's side the map is (255, 127, 255) and the shader changes nothing there.
  - Shader plan (`out/work/nep/cirrus/apply2.py`, TypeScript checked in a scratch worktree, GLSL not yet run): `uCirrus` (amount times (1 - home), bend, shadow gain, opacity floor), `uClump` (t0, t1, t2); the cloud direction and both shadow directions are turned in latitude by `bend * (g * 255 / 127 - 1) * cos(lat)`; `keep = red * smoothstep(t0, t1, blue) * mix(floor, 1, smoothstep(t1, t2, blue))`; alpha and shadow times keep; shadow times `1 + gain * farSide` (farSide = 1 - smoothstep(0.9, 1, blue)). With amount 0 the old expressions run unchanged (the changes sit in two `for` guards). Start values `look.cirrus = { amount: 1, bend: 0.018, shadow: 1.5, floor: 0.5, clump: [0.15, 0.36, 0.7] }`; all of these are tuned on the render, no new take needed for them.
  - Map space preview (`out/work/nep/cirrus/pv5.py DIR bend t0 t1 t2 floor TAG`, `t5/pv-a-before.jpg`, `pv-a-p1.jpg`, `pv-b-*.jpg`) with clump [0.27, 0.45, 0.72]: scratches gone, chains in pieces with tapered ends; but it cut 50 to 64 percent of the cover, so the start values above are lower. The preview is about 1.5 to 2 times larger than the render (the globe's radius at 5006 is about 427 px: 0.33 px per texel at the disc's centre, less toward the limb), so judge on the render.

It changes nothing in hold, dive and terrain frames; the judged rise frame gets a slightly higher sky lift (0.74). A finding from the Saturn agent to check in the own shaders: the harness GPU shades 2 by 2 pixel quads; a pixel that hit `discard` stops storing to variables, so a local first assigned after the discard holds stale memory, and `dFdx`, `dFdy`, `fwidth` and `texture()` without an explicit level read it for the live neighbours. RULE: no `discard` before the last statement of a fragment shader (carry a flag and discard at the end), and no derivative or implicit level `texture()` on a value first assigned inside a per pixel branch. If found, fix with the next commit and say in the next READY which frames changed and by how many pixels. G3 asks for pixel identity across fresh page loads.

Offset (tangent units; the frame is 0.268 high from the centre at 30 degrees): rise -0.137 at 4979 (terrain), -0.085 at 4980 (orbit), a step of 0.052 = 105 px. Dive: -0.165 at 4742, -0.183 at 4743, a step of 0.018 = 37 px. The engine's `horizon()` for the terrain view is the flat world formula of the Mars relief (`terrain.horizonDeg` 8.22 and the dip, fixed numbers), so it does not follow the camera's height over my sphere: it says 7.8 degrees above the centre at 4979 where the limb of the close sphere stands at 5.00 (path table), and 10.4 at 4743 where it stands at 9.35. Seen on the pictures (`/tmp/hz.jpg`: 4975 and 4749 with both lines drawn): the true line lies on the limb, the engine's line 60 to 80 px above it at 4975 and 18 px above it at 4749.
  - Cure (my folder, `plan.ts`): my `horizon()` override returns, for the terrain view, the horizon of the close sphere from the camera (the same law the engine uses for the orbit view: nadir = centre of the sphere, alpha = asin(radius / distance)). True offsets: -0.0875 at 4979 against -0.0852 at 4980; -0.1647 at 4743 against -0.165 at 4742. It moves the cover band on the terrain side of both covers (4743 to 4758, 4973 to 4979); the orbit sides keep the engine's law, so their frames do not change.

For the record, should a longer rise window ever be wanted: `coverWindow: { rise: { seconds: 1 } }` would end the window at 4994, and the engine starts the rise's sky lift at the window's last frame (`film.ts`: `ramp((w.lastFrame + 1) / fps, segment.end, time)`), so the lift at the judged frame 5006 would fall from 0.74 of its weight to 0.53. The weights are the Builder's; the window is left alone.
  - A trap found while curing fault 1: `dive()` reads `this.horizon('terrain', ...)` for `entryBank` (the bank the orbit side of the dive rolls to). With the true horizon in the override the orbit side of the dive would have rolled to another bank (normals 0.173, 0.166, 0.161 at 4740 to 4742 in place of 0.165, 0.161, 0.158).   - Cause: this branch's ring grain (`ring.ts`, `uGrainShape.w = grain * (1 - home)`, made for the rise where the ring is near). In the ending `home` is 0, so the grain is full, and at the lookback's distance it reads as speckle over the ringlets (native pair `/tmp/m5235-upper.png`: the ending's crisp ringlets left, the merge right). `ring.ts` merged with no conflict, so git did not show it.
  - Cure for the merge (not yet applied, the chain is rendering): in `Ring.ending`, after `update`, `uGrainShape.w *= 1 - end`. `end` is 0 in every frame of the stop's own segments (same bytes there) and rises over the first quarter of the retreat (5025 to 5055).
- 10:31, MERGE PROOF, second finding (final 5085 on the scratch merge against the ending's final of 33a5902): 158,528 pixels differ, 72,205 by over 2 levels, 25,907 by 8 or more, largest 124. The night side numbers are the same (ring lit zone 45, 43, 44, 40, 42 blue; column x 524 the same but one value by 1). Where: the ring (grain as streaks under the shutter, `/tmp/m5085-ring.png`) and the day side (`/tmp/m5085-day.png`): the white cloud at x 615 to 722, y 469 to 539 is dimmer (603 pixels over 20 levels), the streaks under the top limb too (441 over 20).
  - Cause on the day side: this branch's cirrus (`uCirrus.x = cirrus.amount * (1 - home)`: the cloud alpha times `keep`, the bend of the cloud direction, the deeper shadow) and flow (`uFlow`, the albedo times the flow map), both full when `home` is 0, and `home` is 0 in the whole ending. The ending's day side was tuned and accepted on main's globe, which has neither.
  - Everything else of this branch is off in the ending by its own rule: `uHold.x`, `uEdgeShape.w`, `uFieldSide.w`, `uLimbDark.x` carry the factor `home`; the near grade is 0 above altitude 1.25 radii.
  - DECISION for the merge: the ending keeps the look the Builder accepted. In `set.ts` `uFlow` and `uCirrus.x` get the factor `(1 - end)`, in `Ring.ending` `uGrainShape.w *= 1 - end`. `end` is 0 in every frame of hold, dive, terrain and rise (a factor of exactly 1, the same uniform values, the same bytes) and rises from 0 at 5025 to 1 at 5055, so the rise hands over to the retreat with cirrus, flow and grain in place and they fade over the first second of the retreat with the rest of the ending's look. Patch as a script: `repo-end/out/work/end/merge/fix.py`. To check after it: 5085 and 5235 against the ending's finals, a draft strip 5015 to 5060 for a pop at the hand over, the draft hashes of the judged frames (`merge/m2.sh`).

### MAIL 149 from the Builder, 12:12 UTC (high), word for word

"Round 32 (the last whole film round, on main 4365a0f = batches 21 and 22, with your ending take in it) is closed: R3 part 5 (frames 5085 and 5235) scored 5, R5 7, R7 part 3 6. Phase 6 has three polish rounds: round 33 takes agent/neptune at the head you name by 13:05 UTC with what is proved; round 34 takes what follows from this mail.

The critics' words on the ending, word for word:
A. R3, major, lookback 5235, rings: "Thin concentric ringlets break into jagged 1-px stair-steps along their arcs at native scale, and the ring segment crossing the upper face of the globe in tile M terminates in an aliased, dead-horizontal knife-edge line" Fix named: "Prefilter or supersample radial ring detail using screen-space derivatives/MIP filtering and soften the planet's ring-shadow boundary with a physically smooth solar penumbra"
B. R3, major, lookback 5235, limb: "The atmospheric rim separates into a doubled edge,a stair-stepped 1-2 px bright blue outer outline detached from the inner limb shading by a darker trough" Fix named: "Integrate atmospheric scattering continuously across the limb with antialiased silhouette coverage so the outer shell merges smoothly into the globe without a separate rim line"
C. R3, major, retreat 5085, cloud deck and the dark storm: "At native 1:1 crop resolution the cloud bands and central dark vortex look soft, blurry, and airbrushed next to the crisp starfield, with smeared texture swirling into the dark oval pit" Fix named: "Increase planetary surface texture resolution or add high-frequency procedural cloud-top detail and filamentary storm structure that holds up at 1:1 native pixel scale"

The last frame is under-lit and does not resolve, so the ending fades out instead of landing." Fix named: "Give the end a final beat. Turn back to show the Sun as a single bright star with the planets lined up, or hold on a well-rim-lit Neptune crescent with the faint Sun beside it, then fade."
F. R7, major, lookback 5242 after the stress encode, night side beside the blue limb: "the dark gradient on the unlit hemisphere breaks into flat macroblock tiles and vertical streaks. The kit's line for the lookback: "The Sun is one bright star among thousands, with the Milky Way behind it. Neptune is a thin blue crescent at the edge of frame."

D, F and G together: the night side at 5235 to 5324 is a real dark hemisphere: faint structure (cloud bands by ringshine and sky light), limb darkening, and a fine deterministic dither on its gradient so that it neither bands in the still nor blocks in the stress encode. Peak level of the night side stays low (the terminator must still read as the edge of the light).
3. E: the crescent itself carries the ending: wider and brighter by a clear step at 5235 to 5324 (a well lit rim with the cloud tops catching light along it), so that the last picture before the fade reads at sheet size as a blue crescent beside one bright Sun. Do not move the camera or the Sun; the ending agent owns the path.

  - What it does not do: `wrap.webp` keeps its bytes, so the trough is still in the map file and would show again in any view that reads the wrap alone with `end` 0 and the hold map off. A hairline of 1 texel is left on the diagonal border in the window (0.16 px at 5235).

  - What it does: it rebuilds the star layer that `specks()` of `patch.py` cut out of the hold patch (round 3), finds its peaks in display levels, and adds back only single points as a Gaussian blob (sigma 2 px, reach 5 px) of the hero's own light, on top of the patch as it is. ### The rise (open item 104), design by numbers, 16:13 to 16:20

- What the engine does (`src/director/moves.ts`, `rise` and `climb`): the cover centre is 4980 (window 16 frames; cover amount 1.0 at 4978 to 4980, then 0.93, 0.81, 0.64, 0.45, 0.27, 0.12, 0.02 at 4987). From the centre the height follows `ascent.pace` as a speed of the log of the height per frame. The camera keeps the top limb 5 to 13 degrees above the middle, so the direction to the planet's centre swings as the disc shrinks under it: the "roll" of -9.5 degrees at 4988 to -101 at 5006 is this, not a camera roll, and it is tied to the height by `climb`. `placed()` adds rigid turns about the planet's centre by time (`turnDeg` 49 over `turnFrames` 24, `placeFrames` 20); left as they are so that 5006 keeps its picture.
- Fixed: the height at 5006 (3.94 radii; R3 judges 5006, so the pull back is at rest there, 0.006 per frame, about 2 px), the pose at 5025 (the ending's), the cover window (the Builder's), the total of 2.57 in the log of the height over the 26 frames from the centre.
- The old pace `{ start: 0.04, ramp: 5, hold: 16, rest: 26, late: 0.006 }`: slow under the cover (0.04 rising to 0.132 over 5 frames), then a constant 0.132 from 4985 to 4996, then an ease out. Fix named: "Soften the dark silhouette and reduce the foreground ring opacity and edge contrast, leaving only the target's faint broken glints."
- minor, pair-01-b.png (neptune.hold 4605), top limb and illuminated right flank (pair-02 crops C, E, F, G and I): "The top rim is more etched and even; down the right flank the atmospheric wash looks thicker and more cyan, with a darker strip separating the lower-right surface from the outer rim." Fix named: "Soften the top rim peak, pull back the cyan wash on the lit flank, and blend the lower-right atmosphere into the surface so it does not read as a separate shell."
- minor, pair-01-b.png (neptune.hold 4605), upper-left moon (pair-02 crop B shows the lit edge): "The moon is slightly smaller vertically and rounder-looking, with less crater relief and a darker, softer left terminator." Fix named: "Match the reference disk silhouette and phase gradient, and restore crisp crater relief on the lit face without over-sharpening the edge."
- minor, pair-01-b.png (neptune.hold 4605), upper-right star core: "A small dark notch interrupts the center of the otherwise matching flare." Fix named: "Remove the notch and keep the target's uninterrupted white core and existing flare rays."

- minor, pair-01-b.png (neptune.hold 4605), right-side background ring and adjacent starfield (pair-02 crops G and I): "The outbound ring strands are a little more sharply drawn and the nearby star points read slightly heavier than in the reference." - major, sheet-08.png (uranus.travel 4455 to 4529), frames 4489-4516, Neptune's moon and the distant Sun: "the moon jumps from near Neptune (4489) to the top-left corner (4495), vanishes (4502), and returns (4509). - minor, sheet-09.png (ending.retreat 5025 to 5144), frames 5079-5101, Neptune lighting: "the lit limb swings from upper left to the right side over about 20 frames while the ring tilt flattens fast. Frame 5362 is the same Milky Way pull-away as 5235 at about half the brightness, a dissolve frozen mid-fade, and the same departure move used at the other stops." - minor, sheet-05.png (film part 1 360 to 4605), all nine holds, especially Neptune lower right: "Signature dives differ, but the spine of every stop is the same centered full-disk portrait and pull-away. Neptune is the only hold cropped off the lower left, so the catalog lens breaks on the last stop." Fix named: "Change the hold angle per stop, and frame Neptune with the same centered full-disk lens as the others."

It is in my frames too: final 4605 (fc69bec), Triton at x 193 to 296, y 216 to 339, largest channel along three rows through the lit right limb: 227, 237, then 0, then 32, 29, 27; 227, 240, 168, then 15, then 29, 25; 212, 213, then 1, then 27, 27. So one pixel at level 0 to 15 right outside the limb against a sky of 25 to 32. Cause: NOT the moon's code. `src/stops/neptune/triton.ts` draws an opaque sphere (alpha 1, no blending), so there is no coverage to composite. It is the sharpening step of the shared lens chain, `src/post/chain.ts` line 118: `c = max(c + uSharpen * (c - around * 0.25), vec3(0.0))` on the developed colour with `sharpen` 0.6. A sky pixel of 27 beside one limb pixel of 237: mean of the four neighbours 79.5, 27 + 0.6 * (27 - 79.5) = -4.5, clamped to 0. The same step gives the dark ring round every bright star (measured at the new points: 54 px darker by 3 to 7 levels) and the boosted limb pixel (237, 240 against 223 to 230 inside). Shared file: not touched, said so. Inside my stop only a softer moon limb (two pixels of fall off) would lower it, and that changes the moon in 4492, 4605, 5006, 5085, 5235 and is not a small proven thing by 18:30.

### R3's severe at 5006 (open item 125), the ring cut and dimmed at the globe's edge: what I know so far (no render yet)

- Geometry at 5006 (`end35/ring5006.ts`): camera 4.94 radii out, disc centre at (676, 485) px, radius 416 px, phase angle 89.6 degrees with the light from the top of the frame, so the lower half is the night side. Ring 1.28 to 1.92 radii, the camera 26.6 degrees over the ring plane, the light 53.2 degrees over it. The near arc of the ring passes IN FRONT of the lower half of the disc (ring at 3.5 to 3.9 radii from the camera, the globe's surface at 4.2 and more), so what crosses the dark disc is really in front, not showing through.
- The ring's own shader has no term that knows the silhouette. Its only planet term is the planet's shadow (`shadow`, distance from the sun axis under 0.985 to 1.025), and at this light the shadow does not reach the ring: the nearest ring point to the axis is 1.28 * sin(53.2) = 1.025. Draw order: planet opaque, ring additive (order 3, depth tested, no depth write), air shell additive (order 5, discarded inside impact 0.995). So in the buffer before the lens the ring's light should be the same on both sides of the silhouette.
- Measured in the final 5006 (`end35/ringstep.py`, `ringstep2.py`, medians B, G, R): sky beside the ring 11, 8, 4; ring off the disc 21 to 27, 15 to 20, 8 to 10; ring on the night disc 17 to 20, 9 to 11, 4 to 5; night disc without ring about 8 to 18, 3 to 7, 1 to 2. Across the silhouette at ring radius 1.45 to 1.75: inside (impact 0.93 to 0.98) 21 to 22, 12 to 13, 6; at 1.01 to 1.03: 32, 24, 13; at 1.08 to 1.10: 25, 18, 10. So the step is about 10 levels and the ring turns from blue to grey white, with a peak right at the limb (the air's line on the night side, about 6, 5, 3 over the ring further out).
- Suspected cause (to be proved with the `ungraded` view, with and without the ring): the lens chain develops with a filmic curve whose toe is convex (`filmic` in `src/post/chain.ts`, about 0.214 x + 17.9 x squared for small x). The same ring light added to the lifted sky (G 8) gives about twice the rise in the picture that it gives over the near black night side (G 3 to 5, R 1 to 2). So the ring reads dimmer and bluer on the disc, and the edge of that is the silhouette. Plus the limb line.
- chain10 (`end35/chain10.sh`, log `chain10.log`): after `== veil review 1` it stops chain9, renders 5006 at draft as `ungraded` and beauty in `repo`, the same two in the second tree with the ring switched off (scratch edit of `set.ts` line 198 in `repo-end`, never committed), mark `== ring diag`; then review 4983 with the veil, G5 and G17, review 4985 and 4982.

### Candidate for the 5006 severe: opacity for the ring (scratch tree `repo-end`, NOT committed, from 18:38)

- `ring.ts`: the ring's blend goes from additive to premultiplied (`CustomBlending`, colour ONE and ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA, alpha ZERO and ONE so the frame's alpha stays the body's own). The shader writes an opacity `solid = uSolid.x * (1 - exp(-density * uSolid.y))` with the density the largest channel of the ring's own strands times its edge, and adds `solid * uSolidGround` to the ring's light (a ring whose light grows with its opacity; the number is the level the lifted sky gives under the ring today, so the ring off the disc keeps its look). With `uSolid.x` 0 the output is the old one (opacity 0, nothing added), so the bytes should hold; to be proved by hash.
- `look.ts`: `ring.solid: { amount: 0.85, density: 125, ground: [0.0052, 0.0087, 0.0108] }` (first take). `set.ts`: one line after `ring.ending(...)`: `ring.solid(rising ? 1 : ending ? 1 - end : 0)`, so the weight is 1 in the rise, falls to 0 with the ending's own look over the first 30 frames of the retreat, and is 0 in the travel, the hold and the dive.
- Known and left: the air shell draws after the ring, so the thin line of the night limb still lies over the near arc.
- chain12 (`end35/chain12.sh`, log `chain12.log`): after `== veil review 2` it stops chain11, renders drafts of 4605, 4707, 4860, 4992, 4996, 5000, 5006, 5012, 5024, 5030, 5040, 5055, 5085, 5235 on the head (`end35/solid/base`) and in the scratch tree (`end35/solid/t1`), then G5 and G17 at review on the head, then review 4985 and 4982 with the veil.
